Quantum Algebra of Chern-Simons Matrix Model and Large N N Limit
Sen Hu, Si Li, Dongheng Ye, Yehao Zhou

Abstract
In this paper we study the algebra of quantum observables of the Chern-Simons matrix model which was originally proposed by Susskind and Polychronakos to describe electrons in fractional quantum Hall effects. We establish the commutation relations for its generators and study the large N N limit of its representation. We show that the large N N limit algebra is isomorphic to the uniform in N N algebra studied by Costello, which is isomorphic to the deformed double current algebra studied by Guay. Under appropriate scaling limit, we show that the large N N limit algebra degenerates to a Lie algebra which admits a surjective map to the affine Lie algebra of 𝔲 ⁡ ( p ) \mathfrak{u}(p) . We conjecture that the large N N limit algebra acts on the large N N limit Hilbert space via the aforementioned degeneration limit, and we prove this conjecture in the p = 1 p=1 case by a detailed study of large N N limit Hilbert space. This leads to a complete proof of the large N N emergence of the 𝔲 ^ ​ ( p ) \widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(p) model as proposed by Dorey, Tong and Turner in the case p = 1 p=1 . This also suggests a rigorous derivation of edge excitation of a fractional quantum Hall droplet.



1
Introduction

Susskind
[
1
]
proposed non-commutative Chern-Simons theory as an effective theory to describe fractional quantum Hall effect (FQHE). The theory describes FQHE as an in-compressible quantum fluid under a large magnetic field
B
. The effective fields to describe FQHE are fluctuations of density of electrons. Relabeling of the electrons is the gauge symmetry of the theory. It becomes area preserving diffeomorphism symmetry of the fluid in the macroscopic limit
[
2
]
.

Like any ordinary field theories one needs regularization to make sense of the field theory. Polychronakos
[
3
]
proposed a matrix model as a regularization of non-commutative Chern-Simons theory which leads to a quantum mechanical system. This matrix model is called Chern-Simons matrix model. The ground state of the theory is related to the Laughlin wave function
[
3
,
4
,
5
]
. In
[
6
]
, a generalized Chern-Simons matrix model with internal degrees of freedom transforming under
U(p)
was considered. This model becomes the original Chern-Simons matrix model when
p=1
. For the non-Abelian case
p\geq 2
, they found the ground states for the matrix model to describe a class of non-Abelian quantum Hall states with filling fraction
\nu=\frac{p}{k+p}
, where
k
is the level of the Chern-Simons matrix model. It is also known that this matrix model is closely related to the Calogero-Moser-Sutherland model
[
3
,
7
,
8
]
.

Recently, the large
N
limit of this quantum matrix model was considered by Dorey, Tong and Turner
[
6
,
9
]
. They showed that this matrix model is closely related to the
d=1+1
WZW conformal field theory, which is supposed to be the boundary CFT of the fractional quantum Hall droplet. In particular, they defined the following operators in this matrix model (see Sections
3.3
and
4.2
for details)
1
1

1

Our notation is different from
[
9
]
. In this paper, we put
\sim
on top of operators to denote rescaled operators.
:

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n}:=\left(\frac{(k+p)N}{p}\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}({{\lambda}^{\dagger}}^{a}Z^{n}\lambda_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}\sum_{c=1}^{p}{{\lambda}^{\dagger}}^{c}Z^{n}\lambda_{c}),\quad n\geq 0

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n}:=\left(J^{a}_{b,-n}\right)^{\dagger},\quad n<0

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{n}:=\left(\frac{(k+p)N}{p}\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mathrm{Tr}(Z^{n}),\quad n>0

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}:=\left(\frac{(k+p)N}{p}\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{n}),\quad n>0

Here
Z,Z^{\dagger}
are
N\crossproduct N
matrix valued operators and
\lambda_{a},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}
are
N

is the level. The

N

\displaystyle[\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\tilde{J}^{c}_{d;m}]\rightarrow(\delta^{a}_{d}\tilde{J}^{c}_{b;n+m}-\delta^{c}_{b}\tilde{J}^{a}_{d;n+m})+km\delta_{m+n,0}(\delta^{c}_{b}\delta^{a}_{d}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{d})\quad\text{when $N\rightarrow\infty$}\quad

(1.1)

In
[
9
]
they provided strong evidence of the above large

Inspired by Dorey, Tong and Turner’s work, we study the algebra of quantum observables of the Chern-Simons matrix model and its representations in the large
N
limit. In order to obtain a better understanding of the commutation relations between the operators
\tilde{J},\tilde{\alpha}
in the
N\rightarrow\infty
limit, we study the full algebra of gauge invariant observables and its action on the Hilbert spaces of the Chern-Simons matrix model, both at finite
N
and in the
N\rightarrow\infty
limit.

Theorem A

The operators

Define the following rescaled operators

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}:=\left(\frac{(k+p)N}{p}\right)^{-\frac{m+n}{2}}\left(e^{a}_{b;m,n}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}t_{m,n}\right)\quad,\quad\tilde{t}_{m,n}:=\left(\frac{(k+p)N}{p}\right)^{-\frac{m+n+2\delta_{m,n}}{2}}t_{m,n}.

Using the generators
\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}

\tilde{t}_{m,n}
and relations of the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}
, a rescaled algebra
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}
can be defined, see Section
3.4
. It turns out that the degeneration limit
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}/(t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}})
captures the large
N
commutation relations of the
\tilde{J}
and
\tilde{t}
operators. More precisely we have the second main result of this paper as follows.

Theorem B

(Theorem
3.2
in the case
\epsilon_{1}=1,\epsilon_{2}=k+p
)
.

The degeneration limit
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}/(t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}})
of the rescaled algebra is isomorphic to the universal enveloping algebra of a certain Lie algebra underlying
{\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
with Lie bracket

\displaystyle[f\otimes A,g\otimes B]=fg\otimes[A,B]+\{f,g\}\otimes\kappa(A,B)\cdot{\bf{1}}_{p},

where
\{-,-\}
is the bilinear pairing on
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{C}^{2})=\mathbb{C}[z,w]
such that

\{z^{n}w^{m},z^{r}w^{s}\}=\delta_{n-m,s-r}(ns-mr)z^{n+r-1}w^{m+s-1}

and
\kappa(-,-)
is the invariant bilinear form on
\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
given by

\displaystyle\kappa(A,B)=\begin{cases}\frac{k(k+p)}{p}\mathrm{Tr}(AB),&A\in\mathfrak{sl}_{p},\\
\frac{1}{p}\mathrm{Tr}(AB),&A\in\mathbb{C}{\bf 1}_{p}.\end{cases}

The isomorphism is given by
{\tilde{J}}^{a}_{b;n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow z^{n}w^{m}\otimes J^{a}_{b}
and
\tilde{t}_{n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow z^{n}w^{m}\otimes{\bf 1}_{p}
. Here
J^{a}_{b}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{p}
is the trace-free part of the elementary matrix
E^{a}_{b}
, i.e.
J^{a}_{b}=E^{a}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}{\bf 1}_{p}
, and
{\bf 1}_{p}
is the identity
p\times p
matrix.

Moreover there is a surjective Lie algebra map from
{\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p}

to the affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(p)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)_{\frac{p}{k+p}}
,
where the latter has generators
\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\alpha_{m},(m\neq 0)
, and Lie brackets

\displaystyle[\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\tilde{J}^{c}_{d;m}]

\displaystyle=\delta^{c}_{b}\tilde{J}^{a}_{d;n+m}-\delta^{a}_{d}\tilde{J}^{c}_{b;n+m}+kn\delta_{n,-m}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\right),

\displaystyle[\tilde{\alpha}_{n},\tilde{\alpha}_{m}]

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{k+p}n\delta_{n,-m}.

The map is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n-m},\quad{\tilde{t}}_{n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\tilde{\alpha}_{n-m},\quad\tilde{t}_{n,n}\mapstochar\rightarrow\frac{1}{n+1}\frac{p}{k+p}.

(1.2)

In this sense, we have observed the emergence of a Kac-Moody Lie algebra in the large
N
scaling limit.

Our next part of the paper studies the large
N
asymptotic behavior of representation of the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}
on the large
N
limit Hilbert space of the matrix model. We have the following.

Theorem C

(Theorem
4.1
)
.

In the Abelian case
p=1
, the generators
\tilde{t}_{m,n}
converge in the large
N
limit (in an appropriate sense). The limit
\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}{\tilde{t}_{m,n}}
are independent of
m-n
, if
m\neq n
, and let

{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m-n}:=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}{\tilde{t}_{m,n}}\quad m\neq n.

Then these operators
\{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{n},n\neq 0\}
form a
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}(1)}_{\frac{1}{k+1}}
current algebra, representing on a bosonic Fock space.

For the non-Abelian case
p\geq 2
, we have the following conjecture (Section
4.2.2
)

Conjecture 1.1
.

There exists an appropriate energy eigenbasis
\mathcal{B}
for the Hilbert space. The operators
\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}
and
\tilde{t}_{m,n}
converge as operators acting on the large
N
limit of the Hilbert space. Moreover in the basis
\mathcal{B}
,
\tilde{t}_{n,n}
has the following asymptotic behaviour in the large
N
limit

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{1}{n+1}\frac{p}{k+p}+O({\sqrt{N}}^{-2})

and the operators
\left\{\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{J}^{a}_{m,n}\right\}
and
\left\{\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{t}_{m,n},m\neq n\right\}
only depend on the difference
m-n
.

If Conjecture
1.1
holds, then together with
Theorem B
, we will have an emergent
\widehat{\mathfrak{su}}(p)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{\frac{p}{k+p}}
Kac-Moody algebra as well as its representations in the large
N
quantum matrix model. Moreover for all
p\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we observe that the central charge of the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
factor

\displaystyle c=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(n+1)\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{p}{k+p}

is equal to the filling factor of the corresponding non-Abelian fractional quantum Hall systems
[
6
]
.

For the
p=1
case, we justify this claim in Appendix
C
, where we derive a rescaled Wigner semicircle law (Proposition (
C.11
)) for the ground state wave function of the matrix model. From this semicircle law, we demonstrate that the central charge
c=\frac{1}{k+1}
of the current algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{\frac{1}{k+1}}
is equal to the filling factor of the corresponding quantum Hall fluid. For the
p>1
case, we provide evidence for this claim in Appendix
C
(see Proposition
C.2
).

Organization of the paper

In Section
2
, we describe the action of the Chern-Simons matrix model (
2.5
) and the gauge symmetry of the model. We point out that this is a Hamiltonian system with constraints.

In Section
3
, we discuss the quantization of the matrix model and give a precise definition of the algebra of quantum observable of the matrix model, which is denoted by
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
. We present a set of generators of the algebra and state our main results for the basic commutation relations for these generators (Theorem
3.1
). Using these commutation relations, we show that all commutation relations can be obtained inductively based on the basic commutation relations. Then we consider the large
N
limit of the commutation relations of the algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
(Proposition
3.2
). This can be viewed as a weak version for the actual large
N
limit, i.e. at the level of algebra.

In Section
4
, we consider the representation of the algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
on the Hilbert space. Restricting to the case
p=1
, we present our final result of the large
N
limit of the commutation relations in the
algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p=1)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
, which is Theorem
4.1
(a strong version of large
N
limit). Our idea can be summarized as follows. Using a suitable basis (
5.45
) of the Hilbert space of the matrix model, which was first proposed in
[
4
]
, we show that the operators in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p=1)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
becomes differential operators on a polynomials ring. We explicitly compute the order of all generators in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p=1)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
in the large
N
limit, and show that all correction terms have lower order. In the non-Abelian case
p\geq 2
, we state several conjectures on the large
N
limit, which are Conjectures (
4.1
-
4.3
).

In Section
5
, we present the proofs of all our results that are stated in previous sections.

In Appendix
A
, we develop a diagrammatic method for performing computations which plays an important role in deriving all these results, especially for Theorem
4.1
, and also Sections
5.2
-
5.3
.

A byproduct of our diagrammatic method is a new diagrammatic proof of the classical Murnagahan-Nakayama rule: a combinatorial rule for the change of basis between the basis of Schur polynomials and the basis of power sum polynomials. The derivation is given in Section
5.2
and Appendix
B
. We expect that these diagrammatic methods will be useful for the study of more general matrix models and quantum systems.

In Appendix
C
, we derive a rescaled Wigner semicircle law for the ground state wave function of the matrix model (Proposition
C.11
). This derivation relies on the moment method in random matrix theory and Theorem
4.1
. Consequently, we justify our claim that the central charge of the large
N
limit algebra is equal to the filling factor of the corresponding quantum Hall fluid.

2
Chern-Simons Matrix Model

The action of the Chern-Simons matrix model was given in
[
3
]
:

\displaystyle S=\int dt\frac{B}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left\{\sum_{m,n=1}^{2}\epsilon_{mn}(\dot{X}_{m}+i[A_{0},X_{m}])X_{n}+2\theta A_{0}-\omega\sum_{m=1}^{2}X_{m}^{2}\right\}+\sum_{a=1}^{p}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(i\dot{\lambda}_{a}-A_{0}\lambda_{a}),

(2.1)

This is a gauged matrix model with a gauge group
U(N)
. Here,
X_{m},m=1,2
are two
N\times N
Hermitian matrices,
p\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\lambda_{a},a=1,\cdots,p
are
N
-vectors, and
A_{0}
is a
N\times N
Hermitian matrix representing the time component of the gauge field. The fields
X_{m},m=1,2
transform in the adjoint representation of the gauge group
\mathrm{U}(N)
, and the
p
vectors
\lambda_{a}
transform in the fundamental representation of the gauge group:

X_{m}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow UX_{m}U^{\dagger},\quad\lambda_{a}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow U\lambda_{a},

where
U\in\mathrm{U}(N)
. Physically
[
1
]
, the system describes
N
electrons on the plane subjected to an external constant magnetic field
B
. The matrices
X_{m},m=1,2
can be interpreted as non-commutative coordinates of the
N
electrons. The average electron density is
\rho_{0}=\frac{1}{2\pi\theta}
. The gauge group
\mathrm{U}(N)
can be understood as an area-preserving diffeomorphism symmetry over the non-commutative plane. The Hamiltonian of the gauged matrix model is given by:

\displaystyle H=\omega\sum_{m=1}^{2}X^{2}_{m},

(2.2)

which represents a harmonic potential confining the electrons near the origin of the plane, where
\omega>0
is the strength of the potential.
Following
[
9
]
, we work with complex variables:

\displaystyle Z:=\sqrt{\frac{B}{2}}(X_{1}+iX_{2})

(2.3)

\displaystyle Z^{\dagger}:=\sqrt{\frac{B}{2}}(X_{1}-iX_{2})

(2.4)

In terms of
Z,Z^{\dagger}
and modulo some terms that are integrals of total derivatives, the action becomes:

S=i\int dt\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left\{Z^{\dagger}(\dot{Z}+i[A_{0},Z])-iB\theta A_{0}+i\omega(Z^{\dagger}Z)\right\}+\sum_{a=1}^{p}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left(\dot{\lambda}_{a}+iA_{0}\lambda_{a}\right)\right]

(2.5)

The matrix model can be viewed as a Hamiltonian system with constraints which is given by variation with respect to the auxiliary field
A_{0}
:

\frac{\delta S}{\delta A_{0}}=-[Z,Z^{\dagger}]+\epsilon_{2}{\bf 1}-\sum_{a=1}^{p}\lambda_{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}=0

(2.6)

Here
\epsilon_{2}:=B\theta
. We will consider quantization of the matrix model in the next section.

3
Quantum Operators

3.1
Operator Algebra

We now consider quantization of the Chern-Simons matrix model. The basic fields are
N\times N
matices
Z=(Z^{i}_{j}),Z^{\dagger}=(Z^{\dagger i}_{j})
, and
N
-vectors
\lambda=(\lambda^{i}_{a}),\lambda^{\dagger}=(\lambda^{\dagger a}_{i})
. Upon quantization, they obey the canonical quantization conditions:

\displaystyle[Z^{i}_{j},{Z^{\dagger}}^{k}_{l}]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{i}_{l}\delta^{k}_{j},\qquad[\lambda_{b}^{j},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{i}]=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{j}_{i}\delta^{a}_{b},\qquad[Z^{i}_{j}\ \text{or}\ {Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{j},\lambda_{a}^{k}\ \text{or}\ {\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{k}]=0.

(3.1)

Here
i,j,k\in\{1,\cdots,N\},\ a,b\in\{1,\cdots,p\}
.
\epsilon_{1}
is the quantization parameter.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})
be the associative algebra generated by the symbols
\{Z^{i}_{j},Z^{\dagger i}_{j},\lambda^{i}_{a},\lambda^{\dagger a}_{i}\}
and a central element
\epsilon_{1}
, subject to the canonical quantization relations (
3.1
).
\{Z^{\dagger i}_{j},\lambda^{\dagger a}_{i}\}
’s are called creation operators, and
\{Z^{i}_{j},\lambda^{i}_{a}\}
are called annihilation operators.

The algebra
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})
carries a natural family of
\mathrm{U}(N)
-action. Such action is generated by the quantum moment map
\mu^{i}_{j}
:

\displaystyle\mu^{i}_{j}:=(:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:+\sum_{a=1}^{p}\lambda_{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}-\epsilon_{2}{\bf 1})^{i}_{j}=:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]^{i}_{j}:+\sum_{a=1}^{p}\lambda_{a}^{i}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{j}-\epsilon_{2}\delta^{i}_{j}.

(3.2)

Here
:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:
is the normal ordered commutator where all creation operators are placed to the left of all annihilation operators in the product. Explicitly,

:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]^{i}_{j}:=\sum_{k}({Z^{\dagger}}^{k}_{j}Z^{i}_{k}-{Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{k}Z^{k}_{j})=\sum_{k}(Z^{i}_{k}{Z^{\dagger}}^{k}_{j}-{Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{k}Z^{k}_{j})-\epsilon_{1}N\delta^{i}_{j}

i.e.,

:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:=[Z,Z^{\dagger}]-\epsilon_{1}N{\bf 1}.

Proposition 3.1
.

The following commutation relations hold

\displaystyle[\mu^{i}_{j},Z^{k}_{l}]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{i}_{l}Z^{k}_{j}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{k}_{j}Z^{i}_{l},

(3.3)

\displaystyle[\mu^{i}_{j},{Z^{\dagger}}^{k}_{l}]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{i}_{l}{Z^{\dagger}}^{k}_{j}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{k}_{j}{Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{l},

(3.4)

\displaystyle[\mu^{i}_{j},\lambda_{a}^{k}]

\displaystyle=-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{k}_{j}\lambda_{a}^{i},

(3.5)

\displaystyle[\mu^{i}_{j},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{k}]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{i}_{k}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{j}.

(3.6)

Proof.

This follows by a direct computation.
∎

As a consequence of this proposition, we find

\displaystyle[\mu^{i}_{j},\mu^{k}_{l}]=\epsilon_{1}(\delta^{i}_{l}\mu^{k}_{j}-\delta^{k}_{j}\mu^{i}_{l})

(3.7)

hold for any
\epsilon_{2}
. Thus
\mu^{i}_{j}
’s generate the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{u}(N)

3
3

3

More precisely,
\mu^{i}_{j}
satisfies commutation relation (
3.7
) and the hermitian condition
(\mu^{i}_{j})^{\dagger}=\mu^{j}_{i}
, so
\sqrt{-1}\mu^{i}_{j}
generates the
\mathfrak{u}(N)
Lie algebra
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
denote the algebra obtained by adding a central element
\epsilon_{2}
to
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})
. Let
J_{N}
be the
left
ideal of
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
generated by
\{\mu^{i}_{j}\}
’s. Define
{\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})^{\mathfrak{g}}
to be the subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
generated by
\mathrm{U}(N)
invariant elements of
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, and let
J_{N}^{\mathfrak{g}}
be the linear subspace of
J_{N}
spanned by
\mathrm{U}(N)
invariant elements of
J_{N}
.

Explicitly, we have

\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})^{\mathfrak{g}}=\{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})|[\mu^{i}_{j},\alpha]=0,\forall i,j\}.

Since
[\mu^{i}_{j},-]
generate an
\mathfrak{u}(N)
-action on
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, one can verify that
J_{N}^{\mathfrak{g}}
is in fact a two sided ideal of
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})^{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Definition 3.3
.

We define the operator algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
as the quotient:

\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}):=\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})^{\mathfrak{g}}/J^{\mathfrak{g}}_{N}.

The algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
can be viewed as the algebra of quantum observables of the matrix model.

Remark 3.1
.

The finite
N
algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is known as the quantization of Nakajima quiver variety
[
10
,
11
]
for the ADHM quiver (see the quiver diagram below)

3.2
Commutation relations

We consider the following
\mathrm{U}(N)
-invariant operators as elements of
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
:

\displaystyle e^{a}_{b;n,m}=\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m})\lambda_{b}\quad\quad n,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

(3.9)

\displaystyle t_{n,m}=\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}))\quad\quad n,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

(3.10)

where
\mathrm{Sym}
denote the averaged total symmetrization. They can be defined via the generating functions

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}=\sum_{n,m}\frac{(m+n)!}{n!m!}u^{n}v^{m}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}))

(3.11)

\displaystyle{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}\lambda_{b}=\sum_{n,m}\frac{(m+n)!}{n!m!}u^{n}v^{m}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m})\lambda_{b}

(3.12)

As we will see, these operators will have a consistent large
N
limit. One of the main objective of this paper is to understand the commutation relations of these operators in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. The results are summarized as follows:

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\epsilon_{3}=\epsilon_{2}-p\epsilon_{1}
. Then we have the following relations in the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
