The derivation of all the above commutation relations will be given in Section 4. Another way to prove these commutation relations is by Calogero representation based on geometric quantization
[
13
,
17
]
. A formula for the general commutators

[e^{a}_{b;n,m},e^{c}_{d;r,s}]

can be derived inductively from the basic commutation relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
). This will be explained in Corollary
3.1
with the help of a filtration structure.

3.3
Large
N
limit algebra

We would like to understand the large
N
limit of the algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. One might have the first instinct that there might be an inverse limit construction such as
\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
N\end{subarray}}{\lim}\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. However, it seems that there is no natural transition map
\mathscr{O}_{N+1}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
when
\epsilon_{2}\neq 0
, even at the classical level (
\epsilon_{1}=0
). The obstruction comes from the moment map equation (
3.2
), since there is no natural way to embed
N\times N
matrices into
(N+1)\times(N+1)
matrices while preserving the
\mathrm{U}(N)
action, and such that the identity matrix
{\bf 1}_{N}
is mapped to identity matrix
{\bf 1}_{N+1}

5
5

5

If
\epsilon_{2}=0
, then we can simply embed the
N\times N
matrices into the first
N
rows and first
N
columns of
(N+1)\times(N+1)
matrices, and embed
\mathrm{U}(N)
into
\mathrm{U}(N+1)
naturally, this induces a surjective map
\mathscr{O}_{N+1}^{(p)}(0,0)\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(0,0)
.
.

Nevertheless, we are rescued by the peculiar fact that all the relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
) are independent of
N
. This enables us to define an algebra by hand, which is independent of
N
and captures all the relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
).

Definition 3.4
.

We define the large
N
limit algebra
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
to be the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-algebra generated by
{e}^{a}_{b;n,m}
and
t_{n,m}
for all
(n,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
and
1\leq a,b\leq p
, subject to relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
). We also introduce the generators

\displaystyle J^{a}_{b;n,m}:={e}^{a}_{b;n,m}-\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}t_{n,m}.

These
J
operators are related to the
\tilde{J}
operators in the introduction by a rescaling, see (
3.32
) below.

By definition, the large
N
limit algebra
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
admits a surjective algebra map

\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})

for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.

Remark 3.2
.

In the work of Costello
[
15
]
, a uniform in
N
algebra, denoted by
\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, was defined as the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-subalgebra of the product
\prod_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
generated by elements

\displaystyle\dot{e}^{a}_{b;n,m}:=\left(e^{a}_{b;n,m}\in\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\right)_{N=1}^{\infty},\quad\dot{t}_{n,m}:=\left(t_{n,m}\in\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\right)_{N=1}^{\infty}.

It was conjectured by Costello in
[
15
]
and proven in
[
13
]
that
\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is isomorphic to the deformed double current algebra studied by Guay
[
14
]
. It follows from definition that there exists a surjective algebra homomorphism
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, mapping
{e}^{a}_{b;n,m}
to
\dot{e}^{a}_{b;n,m}
and
{t}_{n,m}
to
\dot{t}_{n,m}
. The proof of Lemma
3.1
below implies that this map is actually injective, thus our large
N
limit algebra
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is isomorphic to the uniform in
N
algebra
\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. An explicit isomorphism between
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
and Guay’s deformed double current algebra is presented in
[
13
]
.

Remark 3.3
.

It will be useful to rewrite (
3.18
) in terms of
J
generators introduced above.

\begin{split}&[J^{a}_{b;1,0},J^{c}_{d;m,n}]=\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d;m+1,n}-\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b;m+1,n}+n\{\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\}\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}t_{m,n-1}\\
&+\frac{n\epsilon_{2}}{2}\{\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d;m,n-1}+\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b;m,n-1}\}-\frac{n}{p}\{\epsilon_{2}\delta^{c}_{d}J^{a}_{b;m,n-1}+\epsilon_{2}\delta^{a}_{b}J^{c}_{d;m,n-1}\}\\
&-\epsilon_{1}\frac{1}{(1+m+n)\binom{m+n}{m}}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n-1}(1+m_{1}+n_{1})\binom{m_{1}+n_{1}}{m_{1}}\binom{m_{2}+n_{2}}{m_{2}}\times\\
&\{(\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{e;m_{1},n_{1}}J^{e}_{d;m_{2},n_{2}}+\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{e;m_{2},n_{2}}J^{e}_{b;m_{1},n_{1}})-(J^{a}_{d;m_{1},n_{1}}J^{c}_{b;m_{2},n_{2}}+J^{a}_{d;m_{2},n_{2}}J^{c}_{b;m_{1},n_{1}})\}.\end{split}

(3.26)

Equality (
3.26
) can also be derived using the generating function. This will be shown by (
5.31
).

Motivated by
[
9
]
, we conjecture that the action of the generators
J^{a}_{b;n,m}
and
t_{m,n}
(in the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
) on the finite
N
Hilbert space of the matrix model has the asymptotic behavior as
O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n})
and
O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n+2\delta_{m,n}})
respectively in the large
N
limit (Conjecture (
4.1
)). Further details regarding this point will be discussed in Section
4.2.2
. We will show that this conjecture is true for the Abelian case
p=1
as a corollary of Theorem (
4.1
).

Motivated by these rescaling, it turns out that in the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, a certain scaling limit can be defined (Section (
3.4
)). We will show that a Kac-Moody algebra emerges in the large
N
limit as a scaling limit of a modified algebra
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
(Theorem (
3.2
)).

It will be useful to define the following degree on the generators:

\mathrm{deg}(J^{a}_{b;n,m})=n+m,\quad\mathrm{deg}(t_{n,m})=n+m+2,\quad\deg\epsilon_{1}=\deg\epsilon_{2}=0.

It induces a grading on the free algebra

\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}:=\mathbb{C}\langle\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},J^{a}_{b;n,m},t_{n,m}:(n,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2},1\leq a,b\leq p\rangle

by letting the degree for a monomial in
\{J^{a}_{b,m,n},t_{m,n}\}

be the sum of degrees of its components. The grading on
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}
induces a filtration

F_{0}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}\subset F_{1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}\subset F_{2}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}\subset\cdots\subset\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}},\quad F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}:=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}[j],

where
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}[j]
is the homogeneous degree
j
component of
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}
. Since
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is a quotient of
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}
, it inherits the filtration from
F_{\bullet}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}
by

\displaystyle F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}):=\text{image of }F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\mathrm{free}}.

The algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is not graded with respect to the above degree assignment, but it has the following crucial property.

Proposition 3.2
.

Commutators in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
can be written as

\displaystyle[J^{a}_{b;n,m},J^{c}_{d;r,s}]\equiv

\displaystyle\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d;n+r,m+s}-\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b;n+r,m+s}+\{\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\}(ns-mr)\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}t_{n+r-1,m+s-1}

(3.27)

\displaystyle\pmod{F_{n+m+r+s-1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})},

\displaystyle[t_{n,m},J^{a}_{b;r,s}]\equiv

\displaystyle(ns-mr)J^{a}_{b;n+r-1,m+s-1}\pmod{F_{n+m+r+s-3}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})},

(3.28)

\displaystyle[t_{n,m},t_{r,s}]\equiv

\displaystyle(ns-mr)t_{n+r-1,m+s-1}\pmod{F_{n+m+r+s-1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})}.

(3.29)

Proof.

According to relations (
3.26
), (
3.19
)-(
3.21
) and (
3.22
)-(
3.25
), the equation
3.27
is true for
(n,m)=(1,0)
and all
(r,s)
, hence also true for
(n,m)=(0,1)
and all
(r,s)
.
3.28
and
3.29
are true for

m+n\leq 3
and all
(r,s)
.

Next we proceed by induction on
n+m
. According to what we have discussed,
3.27
is true for

n+m\leq 1
and all
(r,s)
. Assume that
3.27
is true for

n+m\leq q
and all
(r,s)
, then

\displaystyle[J^{a}_{b;q+1,0},J^{c}_{d;r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}[[t_{2,1},J^{a}_{b;q,0}],J^{c}_{d;r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}([t_{2,1},[J^{a}_{b;q,0},J^{c}_{d;r,s}]]-[J^{a}_{b;q,0},[t_{2,1},J^{c}_{d;r,s}]])

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{q}[t_{2,1},\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d;q+r,s}-\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b;q+r,s}+\{\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\}sq\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}t_{q+r-1,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s-1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{q}[J^{a}_{b;q,0},(2s-r)J^{c}_{d;r+1,s}+\text{terms in }F_{r+s}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle=\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d;q+1+r,s}-\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b;q+1+r,s}+\{\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\}(q+1)s\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}t_{q+r,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}),

so
3.27
is true for
(n,m)=(q+1,0)
and all
(r,s)
. Using the adjoint action of
t_{0,2}
, we see that
3.27
is true
for
n+m\leq q+1
and all
(r,s)
. By induction on
s
, we see that
3.27
is true for all
n,m,r,s
.

According to what we have discussed,
3.28
is true for

n+m\leq 3
and all
(r,s)
. Assume that
3.28
is true for

n+m\leq q
and all
(r,s)
, then

\displaystyle[t_{q+1,0},J^{a}_{b;r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}[[t_{2,1},t_{q,0}],J^{a}_{b;r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}([t_{2,1},[t_{q,0},J^{a}_{b;r,s}]]-[t_{q,0},[t_{2,1},J^{a}_{b;r,s}]])

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{q}[t_{2,1},qsJ^{a}_{b;q+r-1,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s-3}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]+\frac{1}{q}[t_{q,0},(2s-r)J^{a}_{b;r+1,s}

\displaystyle~+\text{terms in }F_{r+s}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle=(q+1)sJ^{a}_{b;q+r,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s-2}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}),

so
3.28
is true for
(n,m)=(q+1,0)
and all
(r,s)
. Using the adjoint action of
t_{0,2}
, we see that
3.28
is true
for
n+m\leq q+1
and all
(r,s)
. By induction on
q
, we see that
3.28
is true for all
n,m,r,s
.

According to what we have discussed,
3.29
is true for

n+m\leq 3
and all
(r,s)
. Assume that
3.29
is true for

n+m\leq q
and all
(r,s)
, then

\displaystyle[t_{q+1,0},t_{r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}[[t_{2,1},t_{q,0}],t_{r,s}]=-\frac{1}{q}([t_{2,1},[t_{q,0},t_{r,s}]]-[t_{q,0},[t_{2,1},t_{r,s}]])

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{q}[t_{2,1},qst_{q+r-1,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s-1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]+\frac{1}{q}[t_{q,0},(2s-r)t_{r+1,s}

\displaystyle~+\text{terms in }F_{r+s+2}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle=(q+1)st_{q+r,s-1}+\text{terms in }F_{q+r+s}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}),

so
3.29
is true for
(n,m)=(q+1,0)
and all
(r,s)
. Using the adjoint action of
t_{0,2}
, we see that
3.29
is true
for
n+m\leq q+1
and all
(r,s)
. By induction on
q
, we see that
3.29
is true for all
n,m,r,s
.

∎

Corollary 3.1
.

A formula for the general commutators

[e^{a}_{b;n,m},e^{c}_{d;r,s}],\quad[t_{n,m},e^{c}_{d;r,s}],\quad[t_{n,m},t_{r,s}]

can be derived inductively from the basic commutation relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
).

Proof.

From the proof of the above proposition. The lower degree terms in (
3.27
)-(
3.29
) can be obtained inductively.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

Although, in principle, a formula for the general commutator
[e^{a}_{b;n,m},e^{c}_{d;r,s}]
can be derived inductively from the basic commutation relations (
3.13
)-(
3.25
), we do not have a closed formula in general.

Remark 3.5
.

In view of Proposition
3.2
, we see that
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
admits PBW-type generators (as
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-module). More precisely, let us define a set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}):=\{J^{a}_{b;n,m},t_{n,m}\>|(n,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\}

(3.30)

and fix a total order
\preceq
on the set
\mathfrak{G}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
. Then define the set of ordered monomials

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}):=\{1\}\cup\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{O}_{n}\>|\>n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0},\mathcal{O}_{1}\preceq\cdots\preceq\mathcal{O}_{n}\in\mathfrak{G}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})\}.

(3.31)

Then we claim that the natural
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-module map from the free
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-module with basis
\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
to
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is surjective. This can be shown inductively for each
F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. For
F_{0}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, it is generated as a
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-module by monomials in
J^{a}_{b;0,0}
, which is a quotient of universal enveloping algebra
U(\mathfrak{sl}_{p})
, thus
F_{0}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is spanned by elements in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
by the usual PBW theorem for the universal enveloping algebra of Lie algebra. Assume that
F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is generated by elements in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
, then every monomial in
F_{i+1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
can be rearranged into non-decreasing order with respect to the total order
\preceq
, at the cost of adding extra terms in
F_{i}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
(by Proposition
3.2
). Thus
F_{i+1}\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is also generated by elements in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
.

Lemma 3.1
.

The
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-module map
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

According to Remark
3.5
, it remains to show that
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is injective. Consider the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-algebra map
\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\rightarrow\prod_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, then it is enough to show that the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}:\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\rightarrow\prod_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})

is injective. We claim that modulo
\epsilon_{1}
,
p
is injective, i.e.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{pr}}:\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\infty}^{(p)}(0,\epsilon_{2})\rightarrow\prod_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(0,\epsilon_{2})

is injective. This claim follows from
[
15
, Proposition 15.0.2]
. In fact, let
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{N}
be the restriction of
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}
to the
N
-th component in the infinite product, i.e.
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(0,\epsilon_{2})
, then
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{N}
maps our generator
e^{a}_{b;n,m}
to
\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{N}}(IE_{ba}JB_{1}^{n}B_{2}^{m})
in the notation of
loc. cit.

6
6

6

The notations for the gauge and framing dimensions of
[
15
]
are related to our notations as follows. Our
p
is
N
in the
loc. cit.
, and our
N
is
K
in the
loc. cit.
, and we do not have odd dimension in the framing node, this means setting
M=0
in the
loc. cit.
.
. Now assume that
f\in\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
such that
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}(f)=0
, schematically written as

\displaystyle f=\sum_{I}a_{I}\mathcal{O}_{I},\quad a_{I}\in\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{2}],\quad\mathcal{O}_{I}=\mathcal{O}_{i_{1}}\cdots\mathcal{O}_{i_{r}},\quad\mathcal{O}_{i_{1}}\preceq\cdots\preceq\mathcal{O}_{i_{r}},

where we sum over finitely many index sets
I=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{r})
. Let
N
be
\max_{I}|I|
, in other word,
N
is the degree of
f
as a polynomial of element in
\mathfrak{G}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
. Since
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{N}(f)=0
, Proposition 15.0.2 of
loc. cit.
asserts that all coefficients
a_{I}
are zero, thus
f=0
.

Next, we leverage the injectivity result from
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}
to
\mathrm{pr}
. Suppose that
g\in\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
and such that
\mathrm{pr}(g)=0
, then the injectivity of
\overline{\mathrm{pr}}
implies that
g
is divisible by
\epsilon_{1}
, i.e.
\exists g_{1}\in\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})
such that
g=\epsilon_{1}g_{1}
. We claim that
\mathrm{pr}(g_{1})
is also zero. In fact, let
\mathrm{pr}_{N}
be the projection of
\mathrm{pr}
to the
N
-th component
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, since
\epsilon_{1}\mathrm{pr}_{N}(g_{1})=0
, and
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1}]
according to
[
15
, Lemma 15.0.1]
, we conclude that
\mathrm{pr}_{N}(g_{1})=0
. This holds for arbitrary
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, thus
\mathrm{pr}(g_{1})=0
. We can keep applying the above argument, and we see that

\displaystyle\ker(\mathrm{pr})\subset\bigcap_{k}\epsilon_{1}^{k}\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty})=0,

in other words
\mathrm{pr}
is injective. This concludes the proof.
∎

3.4
Scaling limit

In this subsection, we would like to understand the commutation relations in a certain scaling limit. Namely, later we will conjecture that the rescaled operators (
4.23
) converge in the
N\rightarrow\infty
limit as operators acting on the Hilbert space of the Chern-Simons matrix model (Conjecture
4.1
). In this paper, we only prove Conjecture
4.1
in the Abelian case
p=1
. Nevertheless, we can still understand the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
in the scaling limit (
4.23
), which is independent from the Hilbert space of the matrix model.

The first thing we notice is that
t_{0,0}=N
in the scaling limit (
4.23
), so we may replace
N
by
t_{0,0}
. Since
t_{0,0}
is central in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, we may regard
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
as a
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},t_{0,0}]
-algebra. We add the inverse square roots
t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}
and
\epsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
to the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
, and consider the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-subalgebra

\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\subset\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})[t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0},\epsilon_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}]

generated by

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n,m}=\left(\frac{pt_{0,0}}{\epsilon_{2}}\right)^{-\frac{n+m}{2}}J^{a}_{b;n,m},\quad\tilde{t}_{n,m}=\left(\frac{pt_{0,0}}{\epsilon_{2}}\right)^{-\frac{n+m}{2}-\delta_{n,m}}t_{n,m}.

(3.32)

Working with the rescaled operators (
3.32
), taking the
N\rightarrow\infty
limit is equivalent to modulo
t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Theorem 3.2
.

\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-module, and

\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})/(t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0})
is isomorphic to the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie algebra

{\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
with Lie bracket

\displaystyle[f\otimes A,g\otimes B]=fg\otimes[A,B]+\{f,g\}\otimes\kappa(A,B)\cdot{\bf{1}}_{p},

(3.33)

where
\{-,-\}
is the bilinear pairing on
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{C}^{2})=\mathbb{C}[z,w]
such that

\{z^{n}w^{m},z^{r}w^{s}\}=\delta_{n-m,s-r}(ns-mr)z^{n+r-1}w^{m+s-1}

and
\kappa(-,-)
is the invariant bilinear form on
\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
given by

\displaystyle\kappa(A,B)=\begin{cases}\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}\mathrm{Tr}(AB),&A\in\mathfrak{sl}_{p},\\
\frac{1}{p}\mathrm{Tr}(AB),&A\in\mathbb{C}{\bf 1}_{p}.\end{cases}

(3.34)

The isomorphism is given by
{\tilde{J}}^{a}_{b;n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow z^{n}w^{m}\otimes J^{a}_{b}
and
\tilde{t}_{n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow z^{n}w^{m}\otimes{\bf 1}_{p}
. Here
J^{a}_{b}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{p}
is the trace-free part of the elementary matrix
E^{a}_{b}
, i.e.
J^{a}_{b}=E^{a}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}{\bf 1}_{p}
, and
{\bf 1}_{p}
is the identity
p\times p
matrix.

Moreover there is a surjective Lie algebra map from
{\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p}

to the affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(p)_{\epsilon_{3}}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)_{\frac{p}{\epsilon_{2}}}
,
where the latter has generators
\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\alpha_{m},(m\neq 0)
, and Lie brackets

\displaystyle[\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\tilde{J}^{c}_{d;m}]

\displaystyle=\delta^{c}_{b}\tilde{J}^{a}_{d;n+m}-\delta^{a}_{d}\tilde{J}^{c}_{b;n+m}+\epsilon_{3}n\delta_{n,-m}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\right),

(3.35)

\displaystyle[\alpha_{n},\alpha_{m}]

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{\epsilon_{2}}n\delta_{n,-m}.

(3.36)

The map is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n-m},\quad{\tilde{t}}_{n,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\alpha_{n-m},\quad\tilde{t}_{n,n}\mapstochar\rightarrow\frac{1}{n+1}\frac{p}{\epsilon_{2}}.

(3.37)

Proof.

Proposition
3.2
implies that the commutators are schematically of the form

\begin{split}[A_{n,m},B_{p,q}]=&[A,B]_{n+p,m+q}+\delta_{n+p,m+q}\kappa(A,B)(nq-mp)\tilde{t}_{n+p-1,m+q-1}\\
&+t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot(\text{Polynomial in }t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\tilde{J},\tilde{t}).\end{split}

(3.38)

where
A,B
are
\tilde{J}
or
\tilde{t}
and we regard
\tilde{t}
as the diagonal
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
part of
\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
. From (
3.38
) we conclude that

1.

\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is spanned as an
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-module by monomials in
\tilde{J}
and
\tilde{t}
.

2.

\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})/(t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}})
is a quotient of the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{C}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p}
with the Lie brackets (
3.33
).

To show the freeness of
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
over the base ring
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
, let us consider the set of generators

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}):=\{\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n,m},\tilde{t}_{r,s}\>|\>(a,b)\in\{1,\cdots,p\}^{2}\setminus(p,p),(n,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2},(r,s)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\setminus(0,0)\},

(3.39)

and fix a total order
\preceq
on the set
\mathfrak{G}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})
, then define the set of ordered monomials

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}):=\{1\}\cup\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{O}_{n}\>|\>n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0},\mathcal{O}_{1}\preceq\cdots\preceq\mathcal{O}_{n}\in\mathfrak{G}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})\}.

(3.40)

The natural
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-module map from the free
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-module with basis
\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})
to
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is surjective, by the same argument as the Remark
3.5
. It remains to show that the natural map
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is injective. According to Lemma
3.1
, the above map is injective after localization to
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
, thus it is injective before localization, because
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]\cdot\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})
is a free module over the ring
\mathbb{C}[t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
. Therefore
\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}},t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{0,0}]
-module with basis
\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})
.

Since the image of
\mathfrak{B}(\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty})
in the universal enveloping algebra
U({\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p})
forms a basis by the usual PBW theorem for the Lie algebra, we see that the surjective map
U({\cal O}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{O}}^{(p)}_{\infty}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})/(t_{0,0}^{-\frac{1}{2}})
is an isomorphism.

Finally, the surjective map to
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(p)_{\epsilon_{3}}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)_{\frac{p}{\epsilon_{2}}}
follows by a direct computation.
∎

4
Hilbert Space

4.1
Fock space and Physical space

Now we consider the representation of quantum operators of Chern-Simons matrix model with matters.

Let
{\cal F}
denote the Fock space generated from the vacuum
\ket{0}
by applying creation operators
Z^{\dagger}
and
\lambda^{\dagger}

to
\ket{0}
. We consider the representation of
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{(p)}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
at level
k
, which means the central element
\epsilon_{2}
takes the value

\epsilon_{2}=k+p.

The
\mathfrak{u}(N)
generators then become

\displaystyle\mu^{i}_{j}=:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:^{i}_{j}+\sum_{a=1}^{p}\lambda_{a}^{i}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{j}-(k+p)\delta^{i}_{j}.

(4.1)

We define the physical states
\ket{\mathrm{Phys}}
to be those elements of
\cal F
subject
to the Gauss law constraints:

\displaystyle(:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:+\sum_{a=1}^{p}\lambda_{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}-(k+p){\bf 1})\ket{\mathrm{Phys}}=0.

(4.2)

The condition (
4.2
) is the gauge invariance condition of the system, also known as the anomaly free condition, where the gauge group is
\mathrm{U}(N)
.

The
\mathrm{U}(N)
gauge invariant condition (
4.2
) can be separated into
\mathrm{U}(1)
and
\mathrm{SU}(N)
parts.
The
\mathrm{U}(1)
part is by taking the trace of
\mu^{i}_{j}
, which gives

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda^{i}_{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{i}\ket{\mathrm{Phys}}=(k+p)N\ket{\mathrm{Phys}}.

This is equivalent to

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{N}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}_{i}\lambda^{i}_{a}\ket{\mathrm{Phys}}=kN\ket{\mathrm{Phys}},

which says that the number of
\lambda^{\dagger}
’s in a physical state is
kN
.

The second condition is the
\mathrm{SU}(N)
invariance. In a physical state, we have a certain number of
{Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{j}
and
{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{i}
acting on
\ket{0}
. Typical
\mathrm{SU}(N)
invariant tensors consist of
{Z^{\dagger}}^{i}_{j}
’s and
{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{i}
’s of the following forms:

\displaystyle C(\{a\},\{n\})=\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{1}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n_{1}})_{i_{1}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{2}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n_{2}})_{i_{2}}\cdots({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{N}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n_{N}})_{i_{N}},

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{n}).

Here
\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}
is the Levi-Civita symbol.
The general form of the physical states are:

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{l_{1}})]^{m_{1}}[\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{l_{2}})]^{m_{2}}\cdots[\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{l_{s}})]^{m_{s}}C(\{a\},\{n\})C(\{a^{\prime}\},\{n^{\prime}\})C(\{a^{\prime\prime}\},\{n^{\prime\prime}\})\cdots C(\{a^{k}\},\{n^{k}\})\ket{0}

(4.3)

where the operators
C(\{a\},\{n\})
appear
k
times to ensure the number of
\lambda^{\dagger}
’s is
kN
.

The Hamiltonian of the matrix model (
2.5
) is

H=\omega\mathrm{Tr}(Z^{\dagger}Z)

(4.4)

which is proportional to the number operator of
Z^{\dagger}
i.e.
\mathrm{Tr}(Z^{\dagger}Z)
.

Remark 4.1
.

The ground states of the matrix model were described by Dorey, Tong and Turner in
[
6
]
. We recap their result here. The ground states are those of the form (
4.3
) which contain minimal number of
Z^{\dagger}
. With these considerations, one can find:

1.

For
p=1
, there is a unique ground state:

\ket{\mathrm{ground}}=[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{0})_{i_{1}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{1})_{i_{2}}\cdots({\lambda^{\dagger}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{N-1})_{i_{N}}]^{k}\ket{0}.

2.

For
p>1
, write
N=mp+q
where
q<p
, and we define operators:

B(n)_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{p}}=\epsilon_{a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{p}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{1}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n})_{i_{1}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{2}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n})_{i_{2}}\cdots({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a_{p}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n})_{i_{p}}.

Then the ground states are:

\displaystyle\ket{\mathrm{ground};a^{r}_{s}}=\prod_{r=1}^{k}[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}B(0)_{i_{1}\cdots i_{p}}B(1)_{i_{p+1}\cdots i_{2p}}\cdots B(m-1)_{i_{N-p-q+1}\cdots i_{N-q}}

(4.5)

\displaystyle({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a^{r}_{1}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m})_{i_{N-q+1}}({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a^{r}_{2}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m})_{i_{N-q+2}}\cdots({\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a^{r}_{q}}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m})_{i_{N}}]\ket{0}

In the case
p>1
, (
4.5
) can be viewed as a
\mathrm{SU}(p)
tensor with indices
a^{r}_{s}
(
r=1,\cdots,k;s=1,\cdots,q
) taking values in
\{1,2,\cdots,p\}
. One can read off the symmetric properties of these indices from (
4.5
). The span of such tensors is an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{SU}(p)
, whose Young diagram is the following

q
k

The ground states of the matrix model have interesting connections to fractional quantum Hall wave functions. See the early works of Susskind and Polychronakos
[
1
,
3
]
, and see also
[
6
]
.

4.2
Large
N
Limit

In this section, we state the main result of the large
N
limit of the representation of
\mathscr{O}^{(p=1)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1}=1,\epsilon_{2}=k+1)
. In the case
p\geq 2
, we state several conjectures on the large
N
limit.

4.2.1
Large
N
limit for
p=1

Proposition 4.1

(
[
4
]
)
.

The elements:

\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{i})\right)^{c_{i}}\ket{\mathrm{ground}},c_{i}\geq 0

(4.6)

form a basis for the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{phy}
spanned by physical states.

Remark 4.2
.

The basis (
4.6
) is a rescaling of the basis found in
[
4
]
. In this paper, we will present an alternative proof of Proposition (
4.1
) based on diagrammatic calculus in Section (
5.3
) and Appendix (
B
).

For simplicity, we will study the infinite-dimensional representation
\mathcal{H}_{N}
linearly spanned by the above elements.
\mathcal{H}_{N}
is a dense subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{phy}
. Explicitly, let us introduce variables
p_{i},1\leq i\leq N
, and we identify the states
\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}
with the monomial
p_{1}^{c_{1}}p_{2}^{c_{2}}\cdots p_{N}^{c_{N}}
. With these identifications, we have an isomorphism of linear spaces

\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}].

Hence,
\mathcal{H}_{N}
is linearly isomorphic to a polynomial ring in
N
variables
\{p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}\}
. We define the operators
\hat{p_{i}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}
as operators on the polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}]
, representing multiplication by
p_{i}
and taking partial derivatives with respect to
p_{i}
, respectively. Equivalently, under the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}]
, we have the following actions:

\displaystyle\hat{p_{i}}\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=\ket{\cdots,c_{i}+1,\cdots}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=c_{i}\ket{\cdots,c_{i}-1,\cdots}

(4.8)

where
\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}=0
if
c_{i}<0
for some
i
. In the following, we will omit the "hat" in
\hat{p_{i}}
to not distinguish the variables
p_{i}
and multiplication by
p_{i}
, i.e
p_{i}=\hat{p_{i}}
.

The basis
(
4.6
)
is an energy eigenbasis for the Hamiltonian (
4.4
). For the purpose of this paper, we simply take
\omega=1
. The Hamiltonian (
4.4
) is simply the number operator for
Z^{\dagger}
. Under the linear isomorphism
\mathcal{H}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}]
, the monomial
p_{i_{1}}p_{i_{2}}\cdots p_{i_{r}}
has energy
\sum_{j=1}^{r}i_{j}
.

To state our theorem on the large
N
limit of the representation, let us define the energy truncation

{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
= the span of energy eigenstates with energy
\leq E
.

(4.9)

Clearly,
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
is a finite dimensional subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
and they will be naturally isomorphic

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}={\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N+1}={\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N+2}=\cdots

(4.10)

for
N
large enough (in fact, for
N\geq E
). The sequence stabilized as
N\in\infty
. We define the large
N
limit Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}^{\leq E}
, for each energy cutoff
E
as the direct limit of the sequence (
4.10
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\leq E}:=\lim_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{\leq E}_{N}

(4.11)

The operator
t_{m,n}
shifts the energy by
n-m
, hence defines

t_{m,n}:{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E+n-m)}_{N}

for every
N
and
E
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Under the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}]
, the operators
t_{m,n}
are represented as differential operators. In the
N\rightarrow\infty
limit, we have the following asymptotic expansions:

\displaystyle t_{m,n}=\sqrt{N}^{m+n}(k+1)^{m}p_{n-m}+O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n-2})\quad\text{if}\quad m<n

(4.12)

\displaystyle t_{n,n}=\frac{1}{n+1}(k+1)^{n}\sqrt{N}^{2n+2}+O(\sqrt{N}^{2n})\quad\text{if}\quad m=n

(4.13)

\displaystyle t_{n,m}=\sqrt{N}^{n+m}(k+1)^{n-1}(n-m)\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n-m}}+O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n-2})\quad\text{if}\quad m<n

(4.14)

[t_{2,1},t_{m,n}]=(2n-m)t_{m+1,n}+\begin{cases}O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n-1}),&\text{if $m+1\neq n$}\\
O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n+1}),&\text{if $m+1=n$}\end{cases}

(4.15)

[t_{1,2},t_{m,n}]=(n-2m)t_{m,n+1}+\begin{cases}O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n-1}),&\text{if $m\neq n+1$}\\
O(\sqrt{N}^{m+n+1}),&\text{if $m=n+1$}\end{cases}

(4.16)

Here the asymptotic behavior is understood as for any fixed and sufficiently large energy truncation. To be precise, for any fixed
E>m+n
, the linear operator

t_{m,n}:{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E+n-m)}_{N}

will have the asymptotic behavor (
4.12
) when
N
is sufficiently large (so
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
will be stable as well). The interpretation for other relations is similar.

Remark 4.3
.

In the following, whenever we talk about large
N
limit, it is understood in the sense of Theorem (
4.1
).

We will prove the above theorem in the next section using an inductive argument. The key to the proof is the explicit representation of
t_{2,1}
and
t_{1,2}
in the basis (
4.6
), which will be computed using diagrammatic calculus in the next section.

Rescaling and emergent current algebra

The matrix model describes a quantum hall droplet with radius
R\sim\sqrt{2N\theta}

[
3
]
. Recall
k+p=k+1=\epsilon_{2}=B\theta
. Following
[
9
]
, we consider the following rescaled operators:

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{m,n}:=((k+1)N)^{-\frac{m+n+2\delta_{m,n}}{2}}t_{m,n}

(4.17)

The basis (
4.6
) is also rescaled:

\widetilde{{\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(k+1)}}\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{i})\right)^{c_{i}}\ket{\mathrm{ground}},\quad c_{i}\geq 0

(4.18)

We define the rescaled differential operators:

\displaystyle\tilde{p_{i}}\widetilde{\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}}:=\widetilde{\ket{\cdots,c_{i}+1,\cdots}}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{p_{i}}}\widetilde{\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}}:=c_{i}\widetilde{\ket{\cdots,c_{i}-1,\cdots}}

Remark 4.4
.

We will justify the choice of this rescaling in Appendix
C
, where we demonstrate that the radius of the quantum Hall droplet is indeed
R=\sqrt{2N(k+1)}
.

With the identification
\mathcal{H}\cong\mathbb{C}[\tilde{p_{1}},\tilde{p_{2}},\cdots,\tilde{p_{N}}]
, from (
4.12
)-(
4.14
), we have the following large
N
limit (in the sense of Theorem
4.1
) of the rescaled operators
\tilde{t}_{n,m}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{m,n}=\tilde{p}_{n-m}+O(\sqrt{N}^{-2})\quad if\quad m<n

(4.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{1}{k+1}\frac{1}{n+1}+O(\sqrt{N}^{-2})

(4.20)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{n,m}=\frac{1}{k+1}(n-m)\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{p}_{n-m}}+O(\sqrt{N}^{-2})\quad if\quad m<n

(4.21)

In the large
N
limit,
\tilde{t}_{n,m}
and
\tilde{t}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
have the same leading term if
n-m=n^{\prime}-m^{\prime}\neq 0
. We define
\tilde{\alpha}_{-n},n>0
to be the leading term of
\tilde{t}_{m,m+n}
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{n},n>0
to be the leading term of
\tilde{t}_{m+n,m}
.
A
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{\frac{1}{k+1}}
current algebra
span\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n},c\}
emerges in the large
N
limit. The generators
\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n},c\}
satisfy:

[\tilde{\alpha}_{n},\tilde{\alpha}_{m}]=cn\delta_{n,-m}

where the central charge is

\displaystyle c=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(n+1)\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{1}{k+1}

(4.22)

The large
N
limit representation of the generators are

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{n}=\frac{1}{k+1}n\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{p}_{n}}\quad\quad n>0

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}={\tilde{p}}_{n}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad n>0

They act on the infinite dimensional bosonic Fock space
\mathbb{C}[{\tilde{p}}_{1},{\tilde{p}}_{2},{\tilde{p}}_{3},{\tilde{p}}_{4},\cdots]
.

We will justify the physical meaning of (
4.22
) in Appendix
C
. In Appendix
C
, we will derive a rescaled Wigner semicircle law for the ground state wave function of the matrix model (Proposition
C.11
). This derivation relies on the moment method in random matrix theory and the second equality of (
4.22
), namely
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(n+1)\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{1}{k+1}
. Consequently, we justify our claim that the central charge of the large
N
limit algebra is equal to the filling factor of the corresponding quantum Hall fluid.

Remark 4.5
.

The key points of the emergent current algebra is summerized below:

•

Let
N
be a large number and
E
be an energy level such that
N\gg E
(More precise meaning of
N\gg E
will be discussed in certain details in Section
5.3
and Appendix
B
). The dimension of the energy eigen-states with energy
E^{\prime}<E
is independent of
N
.

•

There exist a choice of (properly rescaled) basis
\mathcal{B}
(
4.18
) for the Hilbert space, and a rescaling (the scaling depends on
N
) of the operators
t_{m,n}
i.e.
{\tilde{t}}_{m,n}
, such that given an arbitrary energy cutoff
E>m+n
, the restriction of
{{\tilde{t}}_{m,n}}
on
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
has a well defined limit as
N\rightarrow\infty
, which is independent of
E
, as long as
E>m+n
. The
N\rightarrow\infty
limit of the operators
{{\tilde{t}}_{m,n}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E+n-m)}_{N}
form a certain (cutoff) algebra representing on the cutoff Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\leq E}
(
4.11
).

•

Since
E
can be taken arbitrarily large, the cutoff
E
and
N
can be both send to
\infty
in such a way that
E\ll N
. The cutoff algebra representing on a cutoff Hilbert space will tend to an infinite representation of a certain algebra. We emphasize that the leading terms of the operators
{{\tilde{t}}_{m,n}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E+n-m)}_{N}
are independent of
E
and
N
in the large
N
limit is a key point here. We simply call these the large
N
limit algebra and the large
N
limit representation of the algebra. In the
p=1
case discussed above the large
N
limit algebra is a
\mathfrak{u}(1)
current algebra, and the large
N
limit representation of the algebra is a highest weight representation of the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
current algebra.

4.2.2
Large
N
limit for
p\geq 2

Let
\epsilon_{1}=1
. Recall
\epsilon_{2}=k+p
and
\epsilon_{3}=\epsilon_{2}-p\epsilon_{1}=k
. We define the rescaled operators

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}:={\left(\frac{k+p}{p}N\right)}^{-\frac{m+n}{2}}J^{a}_{b;m,n}\quad\quad\tilde{t}_{m,n}:={\left(\frac{k+p}{p}N\right)}^{-\frac{m+n+2\delta_{m,n}}{2}}t_{m,n}

(4.23)

Similar to the
p=1
case,
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
is a finite dimensional
7
7

7

Recall that
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
is generated by
U(N)
-invariant tensors consisting of
Z^{\dagger}
and
\lambda^{\dagger}
. The number of
Z^{\dagger}
is bounded above by
E
. Therefore, the dimension of
{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}
is finite.
subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
and they will be naturally isomorphic

{\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N}={\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N+p}={\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N+2p}={\mathcal{H}}^{(\leq E)}_{N+3p}=\cdots

for
N
large enough. Hence, it is possible that for general
p\geq 2
, the large
N
limit also make sense (in the sense of Theorem
4.1
). We have the following conjectures.

Conjecture 4.1
.

There exists an appropriate energy eigenbasis
\mathcal{B}
for the Hilbert space. The operators
\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}
and
\tilde{t}_{m,n}
converge as operators acting on the large
N
limit of the Hilbert space. Moreover in the basis
\mathcal{B}
,
\tilde{t}_{n,n}
has the following asymptotic behaviour in the large
N
limit

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{n,n}=\frac{1}{n+1}\frac{p}{k+p}+O({\sqrt{N}}^{-2})

(4.24)

and the leading terms of
\{\tilde{J}^{a}_{m,n}\}
and
\{\tilde{t}_{m,n},m\neq n\}
only depend on the difference
m-n
.

Conjecture 4.2
.

We have the following convergence as operators acting on the Hilbert spaces in the large
N
limit

\displaystyle[\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n,m},\tilde{J}^{c}_{d;r,s}]\rightarrow

\displaystyle\delta^{c}_{b}\tilde{J}^{a}_{d;n+r,m+s}-\delta^{a}_{d}\tilde{J}^{c}_{b;n+r,m+s}+k\delta_{n+r,m+s}\{\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\}(ns-mr)\frac{k+p}{p}\tilde{t}_{n+r-1,m+s-1}

\displaystyle[\tilde{t}_{n,m},\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;r,s}]\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle[\tilde{t}_{n,m},\tilde{t}_{r,s}]\rightarrow

\displaystyle\delta_{n+r,m+s}(ns-mr)\tilde{t}_{n+r-1,m+s-1}

Proposition 4.2
.

Conjecture
4.1
implies Conjecture
4.2
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
3.2
. After rescaling, the lower degree terms in
3.27
-
3.29
converge to zero.
∎

Provided that Conjecture
4.1
is true, we define

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m-n}:=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;m,n}

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{m-n}:=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{t}_{m,n}\quad\text{for}\quad m\neq n

From Theorem (
3.2
), Conjecture
4.1
implies the following

Conjecture 4.3
.

We have the following convergence as operators acting on the Hilbert spaces in the large
N
limit

\displaystyle[\tilde{J}^{a}_{b;n},\tilde{J}^{c}_{d;m}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\delta^{c}_{b}\tilde{J}^{a}_{d;n+m}-\delta^{a}_{d}\tilde{J}^{c}_{b;n+m}+kn\delta_{n,-m}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{c}_{d}\delta^{a}_{b}\right),

\displaystyle[\tilde{\alpha}_{n},\tilde{\alpha}_{m}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{p}{k+p}n\delta_{n,-m}.

To summarize, Conjecture
4.1
implies Conjecture
4.2
and Conjecture
4.3
. This is essentially Theorem (
3.2
) which says that Kac-Moody algebra is a quotient of the large
N
limit of a rescaling alegbra. If Conjecture
4.1
is true. We have an emergent
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(p)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)_{\frac{p}{k+p}}
Kac-Moody algebra as well as its representation in the large
N
quantum theory.
As a corollary of Theorem
4.1
, Conjecture
4.1
-
4.3
are true in the Abelian case
p=1
.

We expect that these are the Fourier modes of boundary excitation of the quantum Hall droplet.

5
Derivation of the main results

In this section we will derive the commutation relations (
3.15
)-(
3.17
), (
3.18
) ,(
3.19
)-(
3.21
), and (
3.22
)-(
3.25
). Let us start with some basic relations in the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
.

5.1
Basic relations for the algebra
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})

Given two matrix valued operators
A=\left(A_{j}^{i}\right),B=\left(B_{j}^{i}\right)
, we define their
ordered commutator
by

\widetilde{[A,B]}_{j}^{i}:=A_{k}^{i}B_{j}^{k}-A_{j}^{k}B_{k}^{i}

For example,

:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:=-\widetilde{[Z^{\dagger},Z]}=[Z,Z^{\dagger}]-\epsilon_{1}N{\bf 1}

One property of ordered commutator is that

\operatorname{Tr}\widetilde{[A,B]}=0\text{. }

Lemma 5.1
.

Matrix elements of
uZ+vZ^{\dagger}
commute with each other, i.e.

[(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})^{i}_{j},(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})_{m}^{k}]=0.

Proof.

Direct computation.
∎

As a consequence, we have the following

Proposition 5.1
.

\operatorname{Tr}\left(\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}:\left[Z,Z^{\dagger}\right]:\right)=0.

Equivalently in homogeneous components

\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}):[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:)=0,\qquad\forall m,n.

Proof.

Using the previous lemma

\displaystyle\widetilde{[uZ+vZ^{\dagger},\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}uZ]}=\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}\widetilde{[uZ+vZ^{\dagger},uZ]}=-uv\frac{1}{1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})}:[Z,Z^{\dagger}]:

The proposition follows by taking the trace of both sides.
∎

Corollary 5.1
.

As elements in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
, we have

\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right)\lambda_{a}{{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}\right)=\epsilon_{2}\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right).

Proof.

By definition of the algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
,
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right)\mu\right)=0
in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
, where
\mu
is the moment map (Gauss law constraints) in (
4.1
). The equality then follows from Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Since

\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right)\lambda_{a}{{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}\right)={{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right)\lambda_{a}+p\epsilon_{1}\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{n}{Z^{\dagger}}^{m}\right),

we derive the following relation from the above corollary.

Proposition 5.2
.

e_{a;n,m}^{a}=\left(\epsilon_{2}-p\epsilon_{1}\right)t_{n,m}

(5.1)

We conclude this subsection with some useful identities in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
:

Proposition 5.3
.

For any two matrix-valued operators (in the adjoint representation of
\mathrm{U}(N)
)
A=\left(A_{j}^{i}\right)
,
B=\left(B_{j}^{i}\right)
in the algebra
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\left(A\left[Z,Z^{\dagger}\right]B\right)=\operatorname{Tr}\left(A\left(\epsilon_{2}-\lambda_{a}{{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}\right)B\right)+\epsilon_{1}\operatorname{Tr}A\operatorname{Tr}B

(5.2)

\displaystyle{{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}A\left[Z,Z^{\dagger}\right]B\lambda_{b}={{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{a}A\left(\epsilon_{2}-\lambda_{e}{{{\lambda^{\dagger}}}}^{e}\right)B\lambda_{b}

(5.3)

Proof.

We prove the first equation, which is equivalent to

\operatorname{Tr}A(\mu+\epsilon_{1}N)B=\epsilon_{1}\operatorname{Tr}A\operatorname{Tr}B.

Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\left(A\mu B\right)=A^{i}_{j}\mu^{j}_{k}B_{k}^{i}=A^{i}_{j}\left[\mu_{k}^{j},B_{i}^{k}\right]=\epsilon_{1}A_{j}^{i}\left(\delta_{i}^{j}B_{k}^{k}-\delta_{k}^{k}B_{i}^{j}\right)=\epsilon_{1}\operatorname{Tr}A\operatorname{Tr}B-\epsilon_{1}N\operatorname{Tr}\left(AB\right),

where in the second equation we have used the moment map equation in
\mathscr{O}_{N}^{(p)}\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right)
. The second equation follows from a similar argument. (A diagrammatic approach to this derivation is provided in Appendix
A
.)
∎

5.2
Derivation of the main commutation relations

It will be advantageous to compute commutators by using the following generating function

\displaystyle F(u,v):=\frac{1}{1-\left(uZ+vZ^{\dagger}\right)}=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(m+n)!}{m!n!}\mathrm{Sym}\left(Z^{m}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n}\right)u^{m}v^{n}

(5.4)

F(u,v)
is understood to be a formal power series in the variables
u,v
. In the following, all expressions are viewed as formal power series in
u,v
whose coefficients are operators in
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})
or
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
. We denote the tensor product of
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})
and
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
with the linear space of formal power series in
u,v
as
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
and
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
respectively.

Proposition 5.4
.

The following identities holds in
\mathcal{A}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
. In the following expressions,
(Z^{i}_{j})
and
(F^{i}_{j})
indicate matrix components of
Z
and
F(u,v)
.
F^{l}
indicates the
lth
power of
F
with matrix multiplication. Here,
F^{l}
is a short hand notation for
F(u,v)^{l}
.

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{l}F=l!\underbrace{FZFZFZ\cdots ZF}_{l\text{ copies of }Z}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{l}F=l!\underbrace{FZ^{\dagger}FZ^{\dagger}FZ^{\dagger}\cdots Z^{\dagger}F}_{l\text{ copies of }Z^{\dagger}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle[Z,F]=vF[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F

(5.7)

\displaystyle[Z^{\dagger},F]=-uF[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F

(5.8)

\displaystyle[(Z^{i}_{j}),(F^{k}_{l})]=\epsilon_{1}v(F^{k}_{j})(F^{i}_{l})

(5.9)

\displaystyle(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l})F^{l}=F^{l+1}

(5.10)

\displaystyle F^{l}=\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-1}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-2}\right)\cdots\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1}\right)F

(5.11)

Proof.

By taking partial derivative of
u
on
F
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{u}F(u,v)=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n-1}(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})^{n_{1}}Z(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})^{n_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})^{n_{1}}Z(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})^{n_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle FZF

By taking the repeated partial derivative of
u
we obtain (
5.5
). Equality (
5.6
) can be obtained similarly. Notice that
[Z,-]
,
[Z^{\dagger},-]
, and
[(Z^{i}_{j}),-]
are derivation operators i.e. Leibniz rule is satisfied. Hence (
5.7
)-(
5.9
) follows from similar computations.
Now we show (
5.10
). By direct computation we have

\displaystyle(u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F=F(uZ+vZ^{\dagger})F=F^{2}-F

Here,
F(1-(uZ+vZ^{\dagger}))=1
is used. Using the above equation, we have

\displaystyle(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l})F^{l}=F^{l}+\frac{1}{l}\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l-1}F^{l_{1}}(F^{2}-F)F^{l_{2}}=F^{l+1}

Finally, (
5.11
) follows from (
5.10
).
∎

Corollary 5.2
.

The coefficient of
u^{m}v^{n}
in
F^{l}
is

\displaystyle(1+\frac{m+n}{l-1})(1+\frac{m+n}{l-2})\cdots(1+\frac{m+n}{1})\binom{m+n}{m}\mathrm{Sym}(Z^{m}{Z^{\dagger}}^{n})

We derive the commutation relations (
3.15
)-(
3.25
) using generating functions.

Proposition 5.5
.

The following equations hold in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
:

\displaystyle[t_{2,0},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]=2v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{b}=2v\partial_{u}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

(5.12)

\displaystyle[t_{0,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]=-2u{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZ^{\dagger}F\lambda_{b}=-2u\partial_{v}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

(5.13)

\displaystyle[t_{1,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]=(v\partial_{v}-u\partial_{u}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}.

(5.14)

Proof.

By direct computations.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

The following equation holds in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
:

\begin{split}&{\left[{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}Z\lambda_{b},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}\left(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}\right)F\lambda_{d}\right]}\\
&=\epsilon_{1}\delta_{b}^{c}\lambda^{\dagger^{a}}\partial_{u}F\lambda_{d}-\epsilon_{1}\delta_{d}^{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}\partial_{u}F\lambda_{b}\\
&+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v\left\{\delta_{b}^{c}\lambda^{\dagger a}\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial v}{1}\right)F\lambda_{d}\right.\left.+\delta_{d}^{a}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1}\right)F\lambda_{b}\right\}\\
&-\epsilon_{1}v\left\{\delta_{b}^{c}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F\lambda_{d}+\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}\right\}\\
&+\epsilon_{1}v\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F\lambda_{b}\right\}\\
&-2\epsilon_{1}^{2}v\delta_{d}^{c}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1}\right)F\lambda_{b}.\end{split}

(5.15)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle[{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}Z\lambda_{b},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{d}]=

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}ZF^{2}\lambda_{d}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}Z\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}v\{{\lambda_{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\}

\displaystyle-2\epsilon_{1}^{2}v\delta^{c}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{b}.

The first line in RHS of above equation is equal to

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\{FZF+vF[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F^{2}\}\lambda_{d}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}\{FZF-vF^{2}[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F\}\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{d}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}FZF\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{1}v\{\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{d}+\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}\}

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v\{\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{d}+\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}

Altogether, we have

\begin{split}&[{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}Z\lambda_{b},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{d}]=\epsilon_{1}\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{d}-\epsilon_{1}\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}FZF\lambda_{b}\\
&-\epsilon_{1}v\{\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{d}+\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}\}\\
&+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v\{\delta^{c}_{b}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{d}+\delta^{a}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}\\
&+\epsilon_{1}v\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{c}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\}\\
&-2\epsilon_{1}^{2}v\delta^{c}_{d}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{b}.\end{split}

(5.16)

Applying Proposition (
5.5
) and (
5.11
) to (
5.16
), we obtain (
5.15
).
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

The following equation holds in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
:

\begin{split}&[\mathrm{Tr}(Z^{3}),{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})F\lambda_{b}]\\
&=3\epsilon_{1}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}{\partial_{u}}^{2}F\lambda_{b}\\
&+3\epsilon_{1}v^{2}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}\partial_{u}F\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\partial_{u}F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{b}\}\\
&-3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v^{3}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+\frac{v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u}}{2})(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{b}\\
+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}(1+\frac{v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u}}{2})(1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F\lambda_{b}\end{array}\right\}\\
&+6\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}^{2}v^{3}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{4})(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{3})(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1})F\lambda_{b}\end{split}

(5.17)

Proof.

Let us compute

\displaystyle{\left[\operatorname{Tr}\left(Z^{3}\right),{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\right]}=3\epsilon_{1}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{F^{2}Z^{2}F+FZ^{2}F^{2}\right\}\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=3\epsilon_{1}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}FZFZF+F[F,Z]ZF\\
+FZFZF+FZ[Z,F]F\end{array}\right\}\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=6\epsilon_{1}v\lambda^{\dagger^{a}}FZFZF\lambda_{b}+3\epsilon_{1}v^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{-F^{2}\left[Z,Z^{\dagger}\right]FZF+FZF\left[Z,Z^{\dagger}\right]F^{2}\right\}\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=6\epsilon_{1}v\lambda^{\dagger^{a}}FZFZF\lambda_{b}+3\epsilon_{1}v^{2}\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}FZF\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF{\lambda}_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{FZF^{3}-F^{3}ZF\right\}\lambda_{b}

The last line in the above equation is proportional to

\begin{split}&{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{FZF^{3}-F^{3}ZF\right\}\lambda_{b}={\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{F[Z,F^{2}]F\right\}\lambda_{b}\\
&~=v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{F^{3}[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F^{2}\right\}\lambda_{b}+v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\left\{F^{2}[Z,Z^{\dagger}]F^{3}\right\}\lambda_{b}\\
&~=-v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}-v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}+2\epsilon_{2}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{b},\end{split}

(5.18)

which implies that

\begin{split}&[\operatorname{Tr}\left(Z^{3}\right),{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=6\epsilon_{1}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZFZF\lambda_{b}+3\epsilon_{1}v^{2}\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}FZF\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\right\}\\
&\quad-3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v^{3}\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\right\}+6\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}^{2}v^{3}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{b}\end{split}

(5.19)

Now apply proposition (
5.5
) and (
5.11
) to (
5.19
), and we obtain (
5.17
).
∎

Next we take the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
part of (
5.17
), and it leads us to the following

Corollary 5.3
.

The following equation holds in
\mathscr{O}^{(p)}_{N}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\![u,v]\!]
.

\begin{split}&[\mathrm{Tr}(Z^{3}),\mathrm{Tr}((1+v\partial_{v}+u\partial_{u})F)]=3\epsilon_{1}v\mathrm{Tr}({\partial_{u}}^{2}F)\\
&-3\epsilon_{1}v^{3}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\}\\
&-3\epsilon_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{3}v^{3}\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}\\
&+6\epsilon_{1}(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})v^{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5}).\end{split}

(5.20)

Proof.

Contract the indices
a
and
b
in (
5.19
) and apply the proposition
{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{a}=\epsilon_{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F)
. We obtain

\begin{split}&[\operatorname{Tr}\left(Z^{3}\right),\epsilon_{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})]=6\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}v\mathrm{Tr}(FZFZF)+3\epsilon_{1}v^{2}\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}FZF\lambda_{a}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}\right\}\\
&\quad-3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}v^{3}\left\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\right\}+6\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}^{2}\epsilon_{3}v^{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5}).\end{split}

(5.21)

The second term in right hand side of the above equation can be computed as follows

\begin{split}&{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}FZF\lambda_{a}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}=[{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}FZF\lambda_{e}]\\
&=\epsilon_{1}p{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}\{F^{3}ZF-FZF^{3}\}\lambda_{a}-\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}^{2}v\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}+2\epsilon_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{3}v\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5})\\
&=\epsilon_{1}pv\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}-2\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}pv{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{a}\\
&\quad-\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}^{2}v\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}+2\epsilon_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{3}v\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5}).\end{split}

(5.22)

From the second line to the third line in (
5.22
), we use (
5.18
). Altogether we get (
5.20
).
∎

We now derive other commutators
[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
,
[t_{1,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
and
[t_{0,3},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
from (
5.17
). Let us first consider
[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
. Notice we have relation
6t_{2,1}=[t_{3,0},t_{0,2}]
.

Denote the operator
[t_{0,2},-]
as
\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}
.
Now we compute

\begin{split}&[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=-\frac{1}{6}[[t_{0,2},t_{3,0}],{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]\\
&=-\frac{1}{6}\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}[t_{3,0},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]-[t_{3,0},\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\}\\
&=-\frac{1}{6}\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}[t_{3,0},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}].\end{split}

Here, we used the Jacobi identity from the first line to the second line. From the second line to the third line, we use
[t_{0,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=-2u\partial_{v}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}
, which follows from the next proposition.

Proposition 5.8
.

For any positive integer
l
, we have

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{l}\lambda_{b}=0

(5.23)

Proof.

(
5.23
) is true for
l=1
. Now, it is easy to see that the operator
\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}
commute with
u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}
. This fact, together with (
5.11
), implies that

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{l}\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-1}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-2}\right)\cdots\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1}\right){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-1}\right)\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{l-2}\right)\cdots\left(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{1}\right)\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}=0

∎

Another useful property is that
\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}
is a derivation, i.e. the Leibniz rule is satisfied when it acts on a product of operators. These properties allow us to compute
\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}[t_{3,0},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]
from the expression (
5.17
). For example, there is a term
\propto 3v\partial_{u}^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}
on the RHS of (
5.17
). We compute the action of
\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}
on such term

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}3v\partial_{u}^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=[\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v},3v\partial_{u}^{2}]{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}=[2u\partial_{v},3v\partial_{u}^{2}]{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle=(6u\partial_{u}^{2}-12v\partial_{u}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}

Another example is

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}3v^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}\partial_{u}F\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle={\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}[\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v},3v^{2}\partial_{u}]{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}

\displaystyle={\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}(12uv\partial_{u}-6v^{2}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}

Similarly, we obtain

\begin{split}&[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=(-u\partial_{u}^{2}+2v\partial_{u}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}\\
&+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}(-2uv\partial_{u}+v^{2}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(-2uv\partial_{u}+v^{2}\partial_{v})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\\
&+3\epsilon_{2}uv^{2}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}-6\epsilon_{2}^{2}uv^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{b}\end{split}

(5.24)

Now we use similar argument to compute

\displaystyle[t_{1,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]=\frac{1}{4}[[t_{2,1},t_{0,2}],{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]=-\frac{1}{4}\{\mathrm{adj}_{t_{0,2}}+2u\partial_{v}\}[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}],

and we find

\begin{split}&[t_{1,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=(v\partial_{v}^{2}-2u\partial_{u}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}\\
&+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}(-2uv\partial_{v}+u^{2}\partial_{u}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(-2uv\partial_{v}+u^{2}\partial_{u})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\\
&-3\epsilon_{2}u^{2}v\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}+6\epsilon_{2}^{2}u^{2}v{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{b}\end{split}

(5.25)

Using
t_{0,3}=\frac{1}{2}[t_{1,2},t_{0,2}]
, and apply similar argument, we obtain

\begin{split}&[t_{0,3},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{b}]=-3u\partial_{v}^{2}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}\\
&+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}(3u^{2}\partial_{v}){\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F\lambda_{b}-{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}(3u^{2}\partial_{v})F\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}\\
&+3\epsilon_{2}u^{3}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{b}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{b}\}-6\epsilon_{2}^{2}u^{3}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{5}\lambda_{b}.\end{split}

(5.26)

Remark 5.1
.

The algebra has symmetry
Z\leftrightarrow Z^{\dagger}
,
\epsilon_{1}\leftrightarrow-\epsilon_{1}
,
\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}\leftrightarrow-\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}
,
\epsilon_{2}\leftrightarrow-\epsilon_{2}
and
u\leftrightarrow v
. As a consistency check, one can see that (
5.17
) and (
5.24
) become (
5.26
) and (
5.25
) respectively under this symmetry.

For the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
part of
[t_{2,1},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
,
[t_{1,2},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
and
[t_{0,3},{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F\lambda_{b}]
, we apply the same argument. From (
5.20
), we obtain

Proposition 5.9
.

\begin{split}&[t_{3,0},\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})]=3v\partial_{u}^{2}\mathrm{Tr}(F)-3v^{3}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\}\\
&-3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}v^{3}\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}+6(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})v^{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5})\end{split}

(5.27)

\begin{split}&[t_{2,1},\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})]=(-u\partial_{u}^{2}+2v\partial_{u}\partial_{v})\mathrm{Tr}(F)+3uv^{2}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\}\\
&+3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}uv^{2}\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}-6(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})uv^{2}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5})\end{split}

(5.28)

\begin{split}&[t_{1,2},\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})]=(v\partial_{v}^{2}-2u\partial_{u}\partial_{v})\mathrm{Tr}(F)-3vu^{2}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\}\\
&-3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}vu^{2}\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}+6(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})vu^{2}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5})\end{split}

(5.29)

\begin{split}&[t_{0,3},\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})]=-3u\partial_{v}^{2}\mathrm{Tr}(F)+3u^{3}\{{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{3}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{2}\lambda_{a}+{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{a}F^{2}\lambda_{e}{\lambda^{\dagger}}^{e}F^{3}\lambda_{a}\}\\
&+3\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3}u^{3}\{\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}(F^{2})\mathrm{Tr}(F^{3})\}-6(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})u^{3}\mathrm{Tr}(F^{5})\end{split}

(5.30)

)-(

), (

), and (

) by taking coefficient of

Finally, we derive
3.26
. We decompose
{{\lambda}^{\dagger}}^{a}F(u,v)\lambda_{b}
into
\mathfrak{sl}_{p}
and
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
parts. Introduce

J^{a}_{b}(u,v):=\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\left({\lambda}^{a}F(u,v)\lambda_{b}-\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}Tr(F(u,v))\right)\quad,\quad T(u,v):=\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}Tr(F(u,v)).

From (
5.15
), using Lemma
5.1
, we have

\begin{split}&[J^{a}_{b;1,0},(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{c}_{d}(u,v)]=\\
&+\delta^{c}_{b}\partial_{u}J^{a}_{d}(u,v)-\delta^{a}_{d}\partial_{u}J^{c}_{b}(u,v)\\
&+2v\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}}{p}\{\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}-\frac{1}{p}\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{d}\}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})T(u,v)\\
&+v\epsilon_{2}\{\delta^{c}_{b}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{a}_{d}(u,v)+\delta^{a}_{d}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{c}_{b}(u,v)\}\\
&-2v\epsilon_{2}\frac{1}{p}\{\delta^{a}_{b}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{c}_{d}(u,v)+\delta^{c}_{d}(1+\frac{u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v}}{2})(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{a}_{b}(u,v)\}\\
&-\epsilon_{1}v\left\{\delta_{b}^{c}J^{a}_{e}(u,v)\{(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{e}_{d}(u,v)\}+\delta^{a}_{d}\{(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{c}_{e}(u,v)\}J^{e}_{b}(u,v)\right\}\\
&+\epsilon_{1}v\left\{\{(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{a}_{d}(u,v)\}J^{c}_{b}(u,v)+J^{a}_{d}(u,v)\{(1+u\partial_{u}+v\partial_{v})J^{c}_{b}(u,v)\}\right\}.\end{split}

(5.31)

By taking coefficient of
u^{m}v^{n}
in (
5.31
), we obtain (
3.26
).

It turns out that using relations (

. Moreover, the terms

Proposition 5.10

(Proposition
4.1
)
.

The elements:

\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathrm{Tr}({Z^{\dagger}}^{i})\right)^{c_{i}}\ket{\mathrm{ground}},c_{i}\geq 0

(5.45)

form a basis for the Hilbert space of physical states.

The basis (
5.45
) is a rescaling of the basis found in
[
4
]
. They found this basis by making connection to Schur and power sum symmetric polynomials. Let
\mathcal{H}_{N}
denote the infinite dimensional representation linearly spanned by the above basis.

We introduce variables
p_{i},1\leq i\leq N
, and identify the states
\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}
with a monomial
p_{1}^{c_{1}}p_{2}^{c_{2}}\cdots p_{N}^{c_{N}}
in the variables
p_{i},1\leq i\leq N
. With these identifications, we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}].
We define operators:
\hat{p_{i}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}
.

\displaystyle\hat{p_{i}}\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=\ket{\cdots,c_{i}+1,\cdots}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}:=c_{i}\ket{\cdots,c_{i}-1,\cdots}

where
\ket{c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{N}}=0
, if
c_{i}<0
for any
i
. Under the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathbb{C}[p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{N}]
, we define operators:
\hat{p_{i}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}
. The operators
\hat{p_{i}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{i}}
are just multiplication by
p_{i}
and partial derivative of
p_{i}
. So we will omit the ’hat’ in
\hat{p_{i}}
and do not distinguish the variables and operators :
p_{i}=\hat{p_{i}}
.
